E-107 | 130 | E.7.34 | Callidice, queen of the Thesprotians, urges him to stay and offers him the kingdom
E-107 | 131 | E.7.35 | Having married Callidice, Ulysses reigns over the Thesprotians
E-107 | 132 | E.7.35 | Ulysses defeats in battle the neighboring peoples who attack him
E-107 | 133 | E.7.35 | When Callidice dies, Ulysses hands the kingdom over to his son
E-107 | 134 | E.7.35 | Ulysses goes back to Ithaca
E-107 | 135 | E.7.35 | There Ulysses finds Poliporthes, a son Penelope has borne him
E-107 | 136 | E.7.36 | Learning from Circe that he is a son of Ulysses, Telegonus sails in search of him
E-107 | 137 | E.7.36 | Coming to Ithaca, Telegonus drives off some of the cattle
E-107 | 138 | E.7.36 | When Ulysses defends them, Telegonus wounds him with a spear tipped with the spine of a sting-ray
E-107 | 139 | E.7.36 | Ulysses dies of the wound
E-107 | 140 | E.7.37 | Telegonus recognizes him and mourns bitterly
E-107 | 141 | E.7.37 | Telegonus takes the body and Penelope to Circe
E-107 | 142 | E.7.37 | There Telegonus marries Penelope
E-107 | 143 | E.7.37 | Circe sends them both away to the Islands of the Blest
E-108 | 1 | E.7.1 | Ulysses wanders about Sicily
E-109 | 1 | E.7.1 | Ulysses wanders about the ocean
E-110 | 1 | E.7.1 | Ulysses wanders about the Tyrrhenian Sea
E-111 | 1 | E.7.38 | Penelope is seduced by Antinous
E-111 | 2 | E.7.38 | Ulysses sends her away to her father Icarius
E-111 | 3 | E.7.38 | Penelope comes to Mantinea in Arcadia (where she bears Pan to Hermes)
E-112 | 1 | E.7.39 | Penelope is seduced by Amphinomus
E-112 | 2 | E.7.39 | Ulysses kills Penelope on that account
E-113 | 1 | E.7.40 | The kin of the slain suitors accuse Ulysses
E-113 | 2 | E.7.40 | Ulysses submits the case to the judgment of Neoptolemus, king of the islands off Epirus
E-113 | 3 | E.7.40 | Hoping to get Cephallenia once Ulysses is out of the way, Neoptolemus sentences him to exile
E-113 | 4 | E.7.40 | Ulysses goes to Thoas son of Andraemon in Aetolia (and marries his daughter)
E-113 | 5 | E.7.40 | Ulysses dies in old age, leaving a son, Leontophonus
